# port-drops — superseded-header symbols that deliberately have no home
# in the new tree.
#
# scripts/check-port-completeness.sh enumerates every public symbol of
# every `_Foo.h` under include/crucible/ (the underscore is the superseded
# marking, so that set is exactly what the project claims to have ported)
# and requires each to exist by name under include/foundation/ or
# include/fixy/.  A symbol that was not carried on purpose is listed here
# with one sentence saying why.  A symbol that is neither present nor
# listed is a missing port and fails the guard.
#
# Each entry is `<header>:<symbol>  [→ <carrier>]  — <one sentence>.`, the
# carrier clause described below being optional except where it is not.
# The header path is
# relative to the include root, so it starts with `crucible/`.  The entry
# is keyed by the (header, symbol) pair.  Every declaration of that name
# inside that header is one row, so a template dropped with its
# specialisations, or a class with its forward declarations, takes one
# line.  A name that several superseded headers declare takes one row per
# header, because a bare name is not an identity.  Keyed by the name
# alone, one row silenced every header that declared it: three unrelated
# runtime_smoke_test functions in three namespaces shared one sentence,
# and the row for the effect aliases silenced the polynomial one.  The
# sentence is the audit surface: write what happened to the symbol, not
# what the surrounding subsystem does.
#
# The carrier clause
# ------------------
# A row may name its carrier between the key and the em dash:
#
#   <header>:<symbol>  → <new-tree header>  — <one sentence>.
#   <header>:<symbol>  → none  — <one sentence>.
#
# The carrier is the header of the new tree that accounts for the type:
# the one that ports it under another shape, or the one that records why
# it was not carried.  The path is repo-relative, starts with a new root
# (include/foundation/ or include/fixy/), and the guard requires the file
# to exist, so a carrier that moves or is deleted fails as MISSING CARRIER.
#
# The clause is REQUIRED when the old header only forward-declares the
# symbol (an opaque class, struct, union or enum declaration, with no
# definition, alias, concept or specialisation of that name in the same
# header).  Such a header never owned the type, so its row is a deferral
# by construction, and a deferral that names nobody lets the ledger stay
# green while the type itself is assigned to no one: completeness per row
# does not compose into completeness per type.  The guard decides this
# from the code, not from the sentence, and reports a row without the
# clause as UNCARRIED DEFERRAL.
#
# `→ none` is the honest form for a type no header carries yet.  Those
# rows are pinned BY NAME in the guard (uncarried_pinned in
# scripts/check-port-completeness.sh): a `→ none` row the guard does not
# pin is UNPINNED, and a pin with no such row is STALE.  The set can only
# change by editing that list, so it shrinks in the same commit as the
# port that gives a type a carrier, and one name cannot silently replace
# another the way it could under a count.
#
# An entry is checked in both directions, so this file drains in lockstep
# with the code, the way the syscall allowlist does:
#   - STALE:    the header no longer declares the symbol — remove the entry;
#   - OBSOLETE: the symbol now exists in the new tree — it is no longer a
#               drop; remove the entry.
# Either is exit 2 from the guard.
#
# To add an entry, run `scripts/check-port-completeness.sh --emit-drops`:
# it prints a template line for every current miss.  Replace the angle-
# bracket placeholder with the sentence.  Do not add an entry to make the
# guard green; add it because the symbol has no business in the new tree.
#
# A gap is not written as a sentence that points at future work.  A type
# the port has not reached is a `→ none` row pinned in the guard, and the
# row drains when the guard reports it obsolete, which is how the
# monotonic clock's gate and clamp came back to fixy/os/Time.h and the
# five named contexts came back to foundation/effects/Ctx.h.

# ── fixy: the grant tier that became the atom tier ──────────────────────
# fixy/Atom.h:17-22 records the renaming: a grant is an effect atom, so
# grant_base, IsGrantTag, which_dim and the tags of eighteen axes became
# the atoms of fixy/atoms/.  Nothing on the atom side carries these names.
#
# which_dim is the one name of this group that ten superseded headers
# declare: _Grant.h declares the primary and the usage tags, and nine
# more specialise it for their own family.  Each of the ten takes a row,
# because the rule an axis registration states is per family.

crucible/fixy/_Grant.h:accept_default_strict_for  — The engagement marker the old resolver demanded; fixy/Fn.h:7-13 takes the strict pole silently for every axis the atom pack does not name, so no marker exists.

# accessors.  The smoke test of each retired with its header.

# ── safety: wrappers ────────────────────────────────────────────────────

crucible/safety/_Affine.h:drop  — The free function became a hidden friend of Qtt (fixy/Qtt.h:175). That is one drop for both grades instead of one per wrapper, and the name can no longer be written for a type that is not a Qtt. An unqualified drop(std::move(x)) still resolves by argument-dependent lookup, and the qualified spelling has no successor. Two mentions in two files use the qualified spelling today, so the move of those consumers to the new tree meets a known list: crucible/fixy/Wrap.h:84 re-exports it with a using-declaration, and test/test_fixy_wrap.cpp:68 calls it through a namespace alias.
crucible/safety/_Linear.h:drop  — The same hidden friend as the affine one; fixy/Qtt.h:175 declares drop once for both grades.
crucible/safety/_Machine.h:machine_transition  — The transition relation is no longer a trait; fixy/Machine.h:13 states it as edges beside the states they join.
crucible/safety/_Machine.h:CRUCIBLE_ALLOW_MACHINE_TRANSITION  → include/fixy/Machine.h  — The new tree spells the edge macro CRUCIBLE_ADMIT_MACHINE_TRANSITION, because the two bodies write into different relations and a second definition of one name stops a translation unit that includes both trees.
crucible/safety/_OwnedMmap.h:is_leak_grant  — The leak witness is now fixy::atom::IsLeakAtom over the leak atom of fixy/atoms/Os.h, as fixy/OwnedMmap.h:20 records.
crucible/safety/_OwnedMmap.h:IsLeakGrant  — The leak witness is now fixy::atom::IsLeakAtom over the leak atom of fixy/atoms/Os.h, as fixy/OwnedMmap.h:20 records.
crucible/safety/_OwnedMmap.h:is_leak_grant_v  — The leak witness is now fixy::atom::IsLeakAtom over the leak atom of fixy/atoms/Os.h, as fixy/OwnedMmap.h:20 records.
crucible/safety/_Refined.h:predicate_implies  — The open implication trait was the hole; fixy/Refined.h:595 keeps implies_v and admits an edge only from the namespace admitted_implications.
crucible/safety/_RefinedAlgebra.h:predicate_implies  — The algebra's own specialisations of that open trait, one per axiom over AllOf, AnyOf and the bounded predicates. Each is now a rule family inside the namespace admitted_implications of fixy/Refined.h:661, which no other header can reopen.
crucible/safety/_ScopedFence.h:mint_scoped_fence  — No band in fixy/Bands.h carries a mint door. The substrate's two-argument constructor ScopedFence{value, {}} is the door, because the grade is a pinned singleton whose whole witness is the braces, so a factory would restate the constructor and nothing else.
crucible/safety/_Secret.h:All  — The policy roster tuple became the namespace admitted_policies of fail-closed edges in fixy/Secret.h:51.
crucible/safety/_Secret.h:kCount  — A hand-written cardinality pin; the roster it pinned is now a namespace the guard enumerates, and such pins were deleted throughout the port.
crucible/safety/_Tagged.h:kAdmittedForwardEdgeCount  — A hand-written cardinality pin on the retag catalog, which is now the namespace admitted_retags; such pins were deleted throughout the port.
crucible/safety/_Tagged.h:kCatalogRosterMatchesCount  — A hand-written cardinality pin on the retag catalog, which is now the namespace admitted_retags; such pins were deleted throughout the port.
crucible/safety/_Tagged.h:kCatalogRosterTuple  — The retag catalog tuple became the namespace admitted_retags of fail-closed edges in fixy/Tagged.h.
crucible/safety/_Tagged.h:WorkloadProfiler  — Any caller can mint a value under a source tag, so the proof of origin of a profiled decision is the class ProfiledDecision in crucible/perf/WorkloadProfiler.h, which only WorkloadProfiler builds.

# ── fixy/os ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
#
# The six rows whose sentence names a successor are renames, not losses.
# The file has one row shape, so a rename reads as a drop whose sentence
# names the new symbol.  A reader follows that name; the guard checks
# only that the old name is absent, which is the limit of this file.
crucible/fixy/_CipherDurable.h:CtxFitsWarmWriterMint  — Ported as CtxFitsDurableMint<Ctx, Stance, Extras...> in fixy/os/CipherDurable.h:116, which folds three textually identical concepts into one parameterised by the stance.

crucible/fixy/_CipherDurable.h:CtxFitsHeadAdvancerMint  — Ported as CtxFitsDurableMint, the same fold; see the warm-writer entry.
crucible/fixy/_Cipher.h:engages_warm_writer_stance_v  — The predicate asked whether a grant pack held the three grants of the warm writer. fixy/os/CipherDurable.h:65 makes the stance one named type, warm_writer_stance, so no pack is left to ask about.
crucible/fixy/_Cipher.h:engages_cold_writer_stance_v  — The same fold as the warm-writer entry. The cold stance is cold_writer_stance at fixy/os/CipherDurable.h:72.
crucible/fixy/_Cipher.h:engages_head_advance_stance_v  — The same fold as the warm-writer entry. The HEAD stance is head_advance_stance at fixy/os/CipherDurable.h:79.
crucible/fixy/_Cipher.h:IsCipherWarmWriterStance  — The concept over the pack predicate. DurableStance at fixy/os/CipherDurable.h:89 constrains a stance type, and CtxFitsDurableMint at :117 gates each mint on the stance it names.
crucible/fixy/_Cipher.h:IsCipherColdWriterStance  — The same fold as the warm-writer concept. DurableStance and CtxFitsDurableMint in fixy/os/CipherDurable.h take its place.
crucible/fixy/_Cipher.h:IsHeadAdvanceStance  — The same fold as the warm-writer concept. DurableStance and CtxFitsDurableMint in fixy/os/CipherDurable.h take its place.
crucible/fixy/_Cipher.h:CipherWarmWriterStance  — The canonical grant pack of the warm writer. The stance type warm_writer_stance at fixy/os/CipherDurable.h:65 names the same open mode, sync and commit.
crucible/fixy/_Cipher.h:CipherColdWriterStance  — The canonical grant pack of the cold writer. The stance type cold_writer_stance at fixy/os/CipherDurable.h:72 pins atomicity::Rename in place of LinkAtomic, which always failed with ENOSYS, as :13 records.
crucible/fixy/_Cipher.h:HeadAdvanceStance  — The canonical grant pack of the HEAD advance. The stance type head_advance_stance at fixy/os/CipherDurable.h:79 names the same open mode, sync and commit.
crucible/fixy/_Cipher.h:stance_pack_satisfies_warm  — A trait that applied the warm-writer concept to a pack alias for the header's own self-test. A stance is a type in the new tree, and DurableStance checks it directly.
crucible/fixy/_Cipher.h:stance_pack_satisfies_warm_v  — The value of the self-test trait above, dropped with it.
crucible/fixy/_Cipher.h:stance_pack_satisfies_cold  — The cold-writer twin of the self-test trait for the warm writer, dropped for the same reason.
crucible/fixy/_Cipher.h:stance_pack_satisfies_cold_v  — The value of the cold-writer self-test trait, dropped with it.
crucible/fixy/_Cipher.h:stance_pack_satisfies_head  — The HEAD-advance twin of the self-test trait for the warm writer, dropped for the same reason.
crucible/fixy/_Cipher.h:stance_pack_satisfies_head_v  — The value of the HEAD-advance self-test trait, dropped with it.
crucible/fixy/_Profile.h:fixy_is_strict  — The switch between the strict gate and the sketch gate. fixy/Fn.h:12 records that the engagement tier, sketch mode and CRUCIBLE_FIXY_STRICT are gone, so no build selects a weaker gate.
crucible/fixy/_Profile.h:IsAcceptedSketch  — The sketch gate accepted every pack, so that a pack without the engagement markers compiled. The new binding needs no markers, because an axis that the pack does not name takes its strict pole (fixy/Fn.h:12).
crucible/fixy/_Profile.h:IsAcceptedActive  — The alias chose the strict gate or the sketch gate. With sketch mode gone, IsAccepted at fixy/Reject.h:177 is the one gate in every build.
crucible/fixy/wrap/_Refined.h:predicate_implies  — A re-export of the open implication trait of safety/_Refined.h, which has its own row. fixy/Refined.h:1269 keeps implies_v, and fixy/Refined.h:858 admits an edge only from the namespace admitted_implications.
crucible/fixy/_Fs.h:CtxAdmitsIoBlock  — Ported twice, because the header separates the two uses the one concept served. fs::CtxAdmitsFs (fixy/os/Fs.h:219) names IO and Block for the three operations that take no atom pack, and fs::CtxAdmitsAtomRow (fixy/os/Fs.h:370) derives the row from the pack for the mint.
crucible/fixy/_Fs.h:TmpFile  — A placeholder for the dirfd surface atomicity::LinkAtomic needed, refused by the old open gate on every call it was given; with LinkAtomic gone it has nothing left to be a placeholder for (fixy/os/Fs.h:27).
crucible/fixy/_Fs.h:Directory  — The tag folded O_DIRECTORY and nothing gated or consumed it; the capability survives, because OwnedFd::open_directory sets that bit itself (fixy/os/Fs.h:27).
crucible/fixy/_Fs.h:NonBlock  — The tag folded O_NONBLOCK and had no consumer anywhere in the old tree (fixy/os/Fs.h:27).
crucible/fixy/_Fs.h:Path  — Two declarations, and neither was carried. fs::flag::Path folded O_PATH and its flag_bits specialisation is the only occurrence of the name in the tree, so nothing selected it, and a descriptor opened O_PATH can be neither read nor written, which is no use to a surface whose every operation reads or writes. fixy/atoms/Os.h:72 keeps the five flag tags that have a consumer. fs::Path was a local alias for the sanitized path, and fixy/os/Fs.h names fixy::Path directly.
crucible/fixy/_Fs.h:LinkAtomic  — A hard ENOSYS in the old commit body, probe-confirmed on the development host, and cold_writer_stance pinned it, so every cold commit failed before a filesystem was consulted; fixy/os/CipherDurable.h:13 pins atomicity::Rename in its place and records the probe.
crucible/fixy/_Fs.h:Msync  — The tag was selectable and always returned EINVAL, and the old comment beside it said a memory-mapped flush goes through the mmap surface instead: no msync call exists in either tree, so that surface was never there. Nothing regressed, and the memory-mapped flush is not implemented.
crucible/fixy/_Io.h:CtxAdmitsIoBlock  — A separate concept of the same name and the same body as the filesystem one. Ported as io::CtxAdmitsAtomRow in fixy/os/Io.h:347, which derives the row from the atom pack rather than naming IO and Block by hand.
crucible/fixy/_Io.h:Synchronous  — An engine tag the old mint already refused, as that header's own self-test asserts; engine_is_io_uring_v is ported with a false primary, so it refuses this tag and any other added later (fixy/os/Io.h:22).
crucible/fixy/_Io.h:Aio  — The same, and the old comment records that review policy forbids libaio (fixy/os/Io.h:22).
crucible/fixy/_Io.h:Splice  — A zerocopy tag the old zerocopy_is_simple_transfer_v refused, asserted false by that header's own self-test; the predicate is ported with a false primary (fixy/os/Io.h:22).
crucible/fixy/_Io.h:MsgZerocopy  — The same shape and the same self-test as Splice (fixy/os/Io.h:22).
crucible/fixy/_Io.h:Default  — A ring_flag tag whose own comment read "no bits set". The old mint accepted it and mapped it to zero bits, so selecting it set nothing. fixy/atoms/Os.h keeps the five ring_flag tags that each set a bit, and a caller that wants no flag passes no flag atom.
crucible/fixy/_Io.h:None  — A zerocopy tag that named the absence of zerocopy. The old mint refused it, and zerocopy_is_none existed to do the refusing, so the tag and its predicate went together. fixy/atoms/Os.h keeps the two accepted tags and nothing else.
crucible/fixy/_Io.h:zerocopy_is_none  — Gone with the zerocopy::None tag it existed to refuse; the surviving conjunct answers false for every tag but the two accepted ones, which the new self-test pins (fixy/os/Io.h:47).
crucible/fixy/_Io.h:zerocopy_is_none_v  — The value spelling of the same predicate, gone with it.
crucible/fixy/_Mmap.h:CtxAdmitsIoBlock  — A third concept of that name and that body, one per os header. Ported as mmap::CtxAdmitsAtomRow in fixy/os/Mmap.h:349 for the two mints, with mmap::CtxAdmitsAdvise (fixy/os/Mmap.h:367) beside it for advise, which takes no atom pack.
crucible/fixy/_Mmap.h:OwnedMmap  — A local alias, one spelling of ::crucible::safety::OwnedMmap inside the mmap namespace. fixy/os/Mmap.h names fixy::OwnedMmap directly (fixy/os/Mmap.h:384), which is the same type reached without a second spelling.
crucible/fixy/_Mmap.h:is_leak_grant  — A specialisation of the old open leak trait for this header's own leak grant, which is how any translation unit could mint the authorization. The witness is now fixy::atom::IsLeakAtom over the leak atom of fixy/atoms/Os.h, which no header can extend (fixy/OwnedMmap.h:20).
crucible/fixy/_Mmap.h:release_aware  — The grant was dead: the old concept read CtxAdmitsIoBlock<Ctx> && is_dangerous_advice_v<Advice> and named neither the grant nor its RegionTag, so the tag was a parameter no gate read. The Permission argument on advise_release_aware is what admits the dangerous advice, and it is still there (fixy/os/Mmap.h:38).
crucible/fixy/_Mmap.h:with_advice  — Nothing lifts an advice tag, because advise takes an Advice directly and never an atom, so the grant was decoration; the advice tags themselves are ported into fixy::mmap::advice in fixy/atoms/Os.h (fixy/os/Mmap.h:25).
crucible/fixy/concurrent/_SpinLock.h:cache_tier_hot  — A namespace-scope constant that restated SpinLock<Tag>::cache_tier, which is ported; nothing read it (fixy/os/SpinLock.h:45).
crucible/fixy/concurrent/_SpinLock.h:fixy_spinlock_runtime_smoke_test  — A smoke function compiled into every including translation unit and called by nothing, which is a shape the port removed throughout; its cases are test/fixy/test_os_spinlock.cpp, which ctest runs.
crucible/concurrent/_SpinLock.h:spinlock_runtime_smoke_test  — A smoke function compiled into every including translation unit and called by nothing; test/fixy/test_os_spinlock.cpp drives the bare lock through each door of the gate.
crucible/fixy/ctrl/_Throws.h:throws  — An alias that filled in the atom's default argument, so a caller could write ctrl::throws for ctrl::throws<>. fixy/Throws.h:32 records the decision as deviation 3: the walk matches the template rather than that one specialisation, and a short name for the specialisation would invite the bug deviation 1 closes. A caller names fixy::atom::ctrl::throws<>.
crucible/fixy/fp/_Polynomial.h:runtime_smoke_test  — An inline smoke test in a public namespace that called each function into a volatile local and asserted nothing. fixy/fp/Polynomial.h:26 records its removal, and test/fixy/test_fp_polynomial.cpp calls each function once against a pinned bit pattern.
crucible/safety/diag/_RowMismatch.h:runtime_smoke_test_row_mismatch  — A smoke function compiled into every including translation unit and called by nothing, which is a shape the port removed throughout; its body is the first case of test/foundation/test_row_mismatch.cpp, which ctest runs.
crucible/safety/diag/_RowMismatch.h:CRUCIBLE_ROW_MISMATCH_ASSERT  → include/foundation/diag/RowMismatch.h  — The new tree spells the macro CRUCIBLE_DIAG_ROW_MISMATCH_ASSERT, because a second definition of one name with a different body stops a translation unit that includes both trees.

# The old row hash carried one partial specialisation per wrapper and
# forward-declared each wrapper and each tier enum so it could name the
# type without including its header. foundation/diag/RowHash.h
# specialises on Graded alone, so it names no wrapper at all and carries
# none of these declarations. Each row below is a forward declaration
# this header made for its own use, not a definition it owned, so each
# names its carrier.  A row with a header after the arrow names the
# new-tree header that accounts for the type.  A row with `→ none` is a
# type no header carries yet: its definition is still in an old header
# nobody has marked, and the guard pins every such row by name.
crucible/safety/diag/_RowHashFold.h:BarrierGuarded  → include/fixy/atoms/Barrier.h  — A forward declaration made so the old fold could specialise on the wrapper without including its header. The new fold names no wrapper, and the wrapper itself was not carried: Axis::BarrierStrength moved to the binding as the atom family of fixy/atoms/Barrier.h over foundation's BarrierStrengthLattice, V301 in fixy/Collision.h reads the strength there, and the value form's only consumer was a using-declaration in the old crucible/fixy/Wrap.h.
crucible/safety/diag/_RowHashFold.h:Consistency  → none  — A forward declaration made so the old fold could specialise on the wrapper without including its header. The new fold names no wrapper, and no header in the new tree carries the wrapper: it is defined in crucible/safety/_Consistency.h, whose rows defer it because its chain order is wrong.
crucible/safety/diag/_RowHashFold.h:CrashClass  → include/fixy/atoms/Ctrl.h  — A forward declaration of the tier enum the old fold read to build a salt's low byte. The new fold reads the lattice type instead, and the crash classes fold into the ctrl atoms of the carrier, where NoThrow is the strict default.
crucible/safety/diag/_RowHashFold.h:FpComplexLayout  → include/fixy/atoms/Fp.h  — A forward declaration of the tier enum the old fold read to build a salt's low byte. The new fold reads the lattice type instead, and the carrier's head comment names complex layout among the seven FpModeLattice settings no collision rule reads, which it does not restate.
crucible/safety/diag/_RowHashFold.h:FpConstantRounding  → include/fixy/atoms/Fp.h  — A forward declaration of the tier enum the old fold read to build a salt's low byte. The new fold reads the lattice type instead, and the carrier's head comment names constant rounding among the seven FpModeLattice settings no collision rule reads, which it does not restate.

The new fold names no wrapper, and the wrapper itself was not carried: Axis::FpMode moved to the binding as the product atom fp::mode of fixy/atoms/Fp.h, which keeps the four settings F101 and F102 can reject on and states in its head comment why the seven no rule reads are decoration, and the value form never had a consumer outside the old dimension and collision tables.

# ── concurrent: the ring predicates and the working-set trait ───────────
#
# Each row is a rename of a shape, not a loss of a check. The new spelling
# is read at every site the old one occupied, and the sentence names it.
crucible/concurrent/_Pipeline.h:pipeline_inline_safe_v  — Renamed is_pipeline_inline_safe_v in fixy/concurrent/Pipeline.h, so the name asks its question, and both pipelines read the new name where they read the old one.

crucible/concurrent/_SpscRing.h:SpscValue  — The old tree carried two textually identical predicates, this one and RingValue in _MpscRing.h, and the port unifies them under the single name RingValue in fixy/concurrent/RingValue.h, which both new rings constrain on.
crucible/concurrent/_WorkingSet.h:has_static_per_call_working_set  — The class template and its one partial specialisation became the concept HasStaticPerCallWorkingSet in fixy/concurrent/WorkingSet.h:85, whose requires-expression is the same witness token for token; Stage.h and Pipeline.h read the concept at all three sites the trait occupied.
crucible/concurrent/_WorkingSet.h:has_static_per_call_working_set_v  — The value spelling of that trait, gone with it, because a concept is used directly where the _v was read.

# ── fixy/os: the time grant tags ────────────────────────────────────────
#
# Nothing outside the old header's own self-test read these four, and
# fixy/os/Time.h states the drop. What gates the time surface now is a
# context concept that reads the context and the clock source.
crucible/fixy/_Time.h:clock_read  — A grant tag no code outside the old header's self-test read; the clock surface is gated by CtxFitsClockReaderMint in fixy/os/Time.h:198, which reads the context and the source rather than a tag.
crucible/fixy/_Time.h:sleep  — A grant tag with the same standing as clock_read, replaced by the CtxFitsBoundedSleepMint gate in fixy/os/Time.h:300, which requires the Block effect and a non-zero bound.
crucible/fixy/_Time.h:tsc_read  — A grant tag with the same standing as clock_read, replaced by the CtxFitsTscReaderMint gate in fixy/os/Time.h:297, which requires a singleton pin proof rather than a tag.
crucible/fixy/_Time.h:which_dim  — The three specialisations that routed those tags onto an axis. An atom carries the axis it engages as a static member in fixy/Atom.h:54, so there is no separate routing trait left to specialise.
crucible/fixy/_Time.h:mint_clock_source  — Removed, not ported.  A clock reading is stamped only by the readers of fixy/os/Time.h, through a private door, right after the read of that clock, so a factory that stamped any value with any source has no new spelling.
crucible/safety/_ClockSource.h:mint_clock_source  — Removed, not ported, for the same reason as the re-export in crucible/fixy/_Time.h: a reading of a clock comes only from a read of that clock, so no factory stamps a value with a source.

# ── safety: one forgeable mint and two inline smoke tests ───────────────
#
# The two smoke tests follow the pattern already recorded above: an inline
# runtime check in a public namespace, moved to a registered test.
crucible/safety/_PipelineStage.h:pipeline_stage_smoke_test  — An inline smoke test in a public namespace; its body moved to test/fixy/test_stage_shape.cpp, which adds a real channel-handle cell the old fakes could not state.
crucible/safety/_SignatureTraits.h:signature_traits_smoke_test  — An inline smoke test in a public namespace; its body moved to test/foundation/test_signature.cpp, which checks each result rather than returning a bool no caller consumed.
#
# The huge-page buffer was the aligned buffer at one alignment, so it folds
# into foundation::AlignedBuffer<T, huge_page_bytes>, and its abort path
# into the one abort of that buffer.
crucible/safety/_HugePageBuffer.h:HugePageBuffer  → include/foundation/AlignedBuffer.h  — Folded into AlignedBuffer<T, huge_page_bytes>, which rounds to the same page, keeps the same abort, and starts each element's lifetime where the old buffer cast raw storage.
crucible/safety/_HugePageBuffer.h:huge_page_allocation_failed_abort_  → include/foundation/AlignedBuffer.h  — Folded into detail::aligned_allocation_failed_abort_, which prints the HugePageAllocationFailed diagnostic at huge-page alignment.

# ── bridges: the machine bridge and the Vigil mode cell ─────────────────
crucible/bridges/_MachineSessionBridge.h:machine_from_session  — A view ends through the handle API of fixy/session/MachineBridge.h: close() at End returns the bridge, and detach() with a reason abandons the remaining steps.
crucible/bridges/_VigilModeHandle.h:mode_transition_allowed  — Renamed mode_transition_allowed_v in fixy/session/VigilMode.h, which reads the edge relation admitted_mode_transitions, so each legal transition is one declared edge.

# ── safety: the crash-class wrapper and its detector ────────────────────
crucible/safety/_Crash.h:CrashClass_v  — An alias of the crash-class enum. The classes fold into the fixy ctrl atoms, and the new tree has no crash-class enum, because nothing in it throws.
crucible/safety/_Crash.h:CrashLattice  — The chain order of the four crash classes. The new tree has no crash-class lattice: a binding that can leave its frame names a ctrl atom.
crucible/safety/_Crash.h:NoThrow  — The crash::NoThrow alias named the strongest class. That class is the strict default of the control-flow axis, so a plain value states it with no wrapper.
crucible/safety/_IsCrash.h:crash_class_v  — This variable read the class of a Crash wrapper. The new tree has no Crash wrapper, so no class is left to read.
crucible/safety/_IsCrash.h:CrashClass_v  — A second spelling of the crash-class alias in the extract namespace. It goes with the alias, which the _Crash.h row drops.
crucible/safety/_IsCrash.h:crash_value_t  — This alias read the payload type of a Crash wrapper. The new tree has no Crash wrapper, so no payload type is left to read.
crucible/safety/_IsCrash.h:IsCrash  — The concept form of the detector. The new tree has no Crash wrapper, so the concept has no type to admit.
crucible/safety/_IsCrash.h:is_crash_smoke_test  — This runtime smoke test used the detector. It goes with the detector.

# ── handles: the file handle ────────────────────────────────────────────
crucible/handles/_FileHandle.h:Fd  → include/fixy/os/Fs.h  — The descriptor value has no twin, because fixy::fs::OwnedFd keeps the number private and only its factories build one.
crucible/handles/_FileHandle.h:FileHandle  → include/fixy/os/Fs.h  — fixy::fs::OwnedFd replaces the owning handle, and its constructor from an int is private, so no caller adopts a descriptor that no factory opened.
crucible/handles/_FileHandle.h:open_read  → include/fixy/os/Fs.h  — mint_file with the ReadOnly mode atom replaces this call, and it demands a context and a sanitized path.
crucible/handles/_FileHandle.h:open_write_append  → include/fixy/os/Fs.h  — mint_file with the WriteAppend mode atom replaces this call, and it demands a context and a sanitized path.
crucible/handles/_FileHandle.h:open_write_truncate  → include/fixy/os/Fs.h  — mint_file with the WriteTruncate mode atom replaces this call, and it demands a context and a sanitized path.

# ── algebra: the lattices that became atoms ─────────────────────────────
#
# Each of these lattices graded one axis of a binding.  The binding now
# carries that axis as an atom of fixy/atoms/, so the lattice type, its
# At<T> singletons and its name function have no successor.  Where an
# atom family needs the enumerators, it restates them verbatim.
crucible/algebra/lattices/_CallShapeLattice.h:CallShape  → include/fixy/atoms/Dispatch.h  — The five call shapes became atoms: Direct is the strict default, and indirect_call, virtual_call and recurses name the shapes above it.
crucible/algebra/lattices/_CallShapeLattice.h:CallShapeLattice  → include/fixy/atoms/Dispatch.h  — The chain over call shapes has no successor, because a binding names its call shape with a dispatch atom and a collision rule reads the atom.
crucible/algebra/lattices/_CallShapeLattice.h:call_shape_name  — A name function for the old enum, and the enum has no successor.
crucible/algebra/lattices/_ControlFlowLattice.h:ControlFlow  → include/fixy/atoms/Ctrl.h  — The five escape tiers became atoms: Pure is the strict default, and abort, throws, longjmp_unsafe and raises_signal name the tiers above it.
crucible/algebra/lattices/_ControlFlowLattice.h:ControlFlowLattice  → include/fixy/atoms/Ctrl.h  — The chain over escapes has no successor, because a binding names each escape with a ctrl atom and a collision rule reads the atom.
crucible/algebra/lattices/_ControlFlowLattice.h:control_flow_name  — A name function for the old enum, and the enum has no successor.
crucible/algebra/lattices/_FpModeLattice.h:CRUCIBLE_FP_LATTICE_VERIFY  — A macro that ran the axiom checks of the eleven sub-lattices, and those lattices have no successor.

crucible/safety/_Fn.h:Fn  — Renamed fixy::fn<Type, Atoms...> in fixy/Fn.h. The nineteen positional grade parameters became a pack of atoms, and an axis the pack does not name takes its strict pole.
crucible/safety/_Fn.h:SecLevel  — The five-level enum is not carried. The atoms as_unclassified, as_public, as_internal, as_classified, as_secret, constant_time and declassify<Policy> in fixy/Atom.h grade the Security axis.
crucible/safety/_Fn.h:UsageMode  — The enum is not carried. The atoms affine, copy, ghost, borrow and capability_usage in fixy/Atom.h grade the Usage axis, and its strict pole is the linear QttGrade::One.

# ── safety: the dimension table ─────────────────────────────────────────
crucible/safety/_DimensionTraits.h:count_wrappers_on_axis  — No successor. One axis has at most one wrapper in fixy::axis_traits<A>::wrapper, so there is nothing to count.
crucible/safety/_DimensionTraits.h:DIMENSION_AXIS_COUNT  — Renamed fixy::axis_count in fixy/Axis.h.
crucible/safety/_DimensionTraits.h:dimension_axis_name  — Renamed fixy::axis_name in fixy/Axis.h, which reads the enumerator identifier by reflection. The hand-kept switch has no successor.
crucible/safety/_DimensionTraits.h:dimension_tier_v  — No successor. It guessed the tier of a wrapper from its lattice, and fixy reads the shape from axis_traits by axis and never from a wrapper.
crucible/safety/_DimensionTraits.h:DimensionAxis  — Renamed fixy::Axis in fixy/Axis.h, with the same 33 enumerators at the same values.
crucible/safety/_DimensionTraits.h:DimensionedGradedWrapper  — No successor. It admitted a graded wrapper that declared its axis in wrapper_dimension, and the new tree keeps no such table.
crucible/safety/_DimensionTraits.h:every_wrapper_kind_has_axis  — A self-check of wrapper_kind_to_axis. every_axis_has_traits in fixy/Axis.h checks that each axis names a fixy::Wrapper value in range.
crucible/safety/_DimensionTraits.h:every_wrapper_kind_has_name  — A self-check of the hand-kept name switch. The new tree reads the name by reflection, so no switch can miss an arm.
crucible/safety/_DimensionTraits.h:FoundationalGrade  — No successor. The test was std::is_object_v, and fixy/Axis.h discharges the Type and Refinement axes by reflection and by contract, not by a grade shape.
crucible/safety/_DimensionTraits.h:LatticeGrade  — An alias of the old algebra::Lattice concept. foundation::algebra::Lattice in foundation/algebra/Lattice.h is the port of that concept.
crucible/safety/_DimensionTraits.h:SemiringGrade  — No successor. foundation/algebra/Lattice.h carries the Lattice and Semiring concepts, and fixy reads the shape of an axis from axis_traits, never from its grade.
crucible/safety/_DimensionTraits.h:tier_admits_lattice  — No successor. It checked the lattice of a wrapper against the tier of its declared axis, and the new tree declares no axis for a wrapper.
crucible/safety/_DimensionTraits.h:tier_admits_modality  — No successor. It admitted every modality that foundation::algebra::IsModality admits, so it checked nothing beyond that concept.
crucible/safety/_DimensionTraits.h:tier_admits_semiring  — No successor. It was the strict form of tier_admits_lattice, and it read the same wrapper table that the new tree does not keep.
crucible/safety/_DimensionTraits.h:tier_for_grade  — No successor. fixy reads the shape of an axis from axis_traits and never infers it from the shape of a grade.
crucible/safety/_DimensionTraits.h:tier_for_grade_v  — The value of tier_for_grade. fixy reads the shape of an axis from axis_traits and never infers it from the shape of a grade.
crucible/safety/_DimensionTraits.h:tier_kind_name  — No successor. foundation::reflect::enum_name in foundation/reflect/EnumName.h reads the name of a fixy::Shape enumerator by reflection.
crucible/safety/_DimensionTraits.h:tier_of_axis  — fixy::axis_traits<A>::shape in fixy/Axis.h gives the shape of each axis. The map is not the old one, because most old Tier S axes are lattices there.
crucible/safety/_DimensionTraits.h:tier_of_axis_v  — The value of tier_of_axis. fixy::axis_traits<A>::shape in fixy/Axis.h gives the shape of each axis.
crucible/safety/_DimensionTraits.h:TIER_KIND_COUNT  — No successor. fixy/Axis.h counts fixy::Shape through std::meta::enumerators_of where it needs the count.
crucible/safety/_DimensionTraits.h:TierKind  — The five tier kinds became fixy::Shape in fixy/Axis.h. The old Tier S chains are lattices there, and only Usage and Staleness keep the semiring shape.
crucible/safety/_DimensionTraits.h:TypestateGrade  — No successor. Session types are not graded, so the Protocol axis of fixy/Axis.h takes the Transition shape and needs no grade concept.
crucible/safety/_DimensionTraits.h:verify_quadruple  — foundation::algebra::GradedWrapper in foundation/algebra/GradedTrait.h checks the lattice and the modality against the Graded substrate. The tier half read wrapper_dimension and has no successor.
crucible/safety/_DimensionTraits.h:VersionedGrade  — No successor. The Version axis of fixy/Axis.h has the Structural shape, a strict pole of version 1 and a contract discharge.
crucible/safety/_DimensionTraits.h:wrapper_dimension  — The table ran from wrapper to axis. fixy::axis_traits<A>::wrapper in fixy/Axis.h runs from axis to wrapper, for the eight wrappers that carry an axis discipline.
crucible/safety/_DimensionTraits.h:wrapper_dimension_v  — The value of wrapper_dimension. fixy::axis_traits<A>::wrapper in fixy/Axis.h keeps the map from axis to wrapper.
crucible/safety/_DimensionTraits.h:wrapper_for  — No successor. fixy::axis_traits<A>::wrapper gives the one wrapper of an axis directly.
crucible/safety/_DimensionTraits.h:wrapper_for_v  — The value of wrapper_for. fixy::axis_traits<A>::wrapper gives the one wrapper of an axis directly.
crucible/safety/_DimensionTraits.h:wrapper_kind_name  — No successor. foundation::reflect::enum_name reads the name of a fixy::Wrapper enumerator by reflection.
crucible/safety/_DimensionTraits.h:wrapper_kind_to_axis  — No successor. fixy::axis_traits<A>::wrapper maps each axis to its wrapper, which is the other direction.
crucible/safety/_DimensionTraits.h:wrapper_lattice_t  — No successor. A graded wrapper exposes lattice_type itself, and foundation::algebra::GradedWrapper in foundation/algebra/GradedTrait.h requires it.
crucible/safety/_DimensionTraits.h:wrapper_modality_v  — No successor. A graded wrapper exposes modality itself, and foundation::algebra::GradedWrapper requires that it agree with its Graded substrate.
crucible/safety/_DimensionTraits.h:wrapper_tier_v  — No successor. It read the tier of a wrapper through wrapper_dimension, and fixy::axis_traits<A>::shape gives the shape by axis.
crucible/safety/_DimensionTraits.h:WRAPPER_KIND_COUNT  — No successor. fixy/Axis.h counts fixy::Wrapper through std::meta::enumerators_of.
crucible/safety/_DimensionTraits.h:WrapperKind  — fixy::Wrapper in fixy/Axis.h names the eight wrappers that carry an axis discipline. The other kinds became atoms or bands, or have their own drop rows.

# ── sessions: the subtype relation, its reasons and the payload order ──
crucible/sessions/_SessionPayloadSubsort.h:is_subsort  → include/fixy/session/Subtype.h  — The specializations of this header were axioms on an open trait. The carrier states each axiom once in the sealed payload_axioms namespace, and a later header cannot add one.
crucible/sessions/_SessionSubtype.h:assert_vendor_subtype_sync  → include/fixy/session/Subtype.h  — Folded into assert_subtype_sync, whose message names the failure class, Subtype_Vendor for a vendor mismatch. VendorPinned now needs the same vendor on both sides, so a Portable protocol no longer stands where a pinned one is required.
crucible/sessions/_SessionSubtype.h:is_subsort  → include/fixy/session/Subtype.h  — The open trait is gone. The payload order is is_payload_subsort_v, which is reflexive and admits only the axioms in the sealed payload_axioms namespace.
crucible/sessions/_SessionSubtype.h:is_subsort_v  → include/fixy/session/Subtype.h  — Renamed is_payload_subsort_v, which reads the sealed payload_axioms and not an open trait.
crucible/sessions/_SessionSubtype.h:is_subtype_sync  → include/fixy/session/Subtype.h  — The trait class is gone. is_subtype_sync_v reads subtype_verdict_v, and is_sync_subtype over SubtypeQuery is the one-argument form that holds an armed cell.
crucible/sessions/_SessionSubtype.h:is_subtype_sync_structural  → include/fixy/session/Subtype.h  — The per-combinator specializations became the refinement preorder of foundation/algebra/Transition.h, one walk over the two type graphs. That walk matches a keyed choice by label and keeps the exits of the supertype, which the positional prefix rule did not.
crucible/sessions/_SessionSubtype.h:protocol_grade_satisfies_v  — Dropped with the protocol grade of _SessionGrade.h, which has no carrier. The new relation compares protocols by the refinement walk alone, with no grade filter.
crucible/sessions/_SessionSubtypeReason.h:assert_subtype_sync_diag  → include/fixy/session/Subtype.h  — Folded into assert_subtype_sync, whose message names the class of the failure and the failed pair.
crucible/sessions/_SessionSubtypeReason.h:is_rejection_reason  → include/fixy/session/Subtype.h  — No recognizer is necessary. subtype_reason_t is SubtypeOk when the pair holds and a SubtypeRejection when it does not, so a reader compares it with SubtypeOk.
crucible/sessions/_SessionSubtypeReason.h:is_rejection_reason_v  → include/fixy/session/Subtype.h  — Dropped with is_rejection_reason. A reader compares subtype_reason_t with SubtypeOk.
crucible/sessions/_SessionSubtypeReason.h:is_subtype_sync_diag_v  → include/fixy/session/Subtype.h  — The reason comes from the same walk as the verdict, so is_subtype_sync_v answers the question that this second walk answered.
crucible/sessions/_SessionSubtypeReason.h:RejectionReason  → include/fixy/session/Subtype.h  — Renamed SubtypeRejection, which is keyed by the mismatch value of the transition algebra and not by a diagnostic class of the catalog.
crucible/sessions/_SessionSubtypeReason.h:subtype_diag_agrees_v  → include/fixy/session/Subtype.h  — It checked that a second walk agreed with the first. The carrier has one walk only, so the two cannot disagree.
crucible/sessions/_SessionSubtypeReason.h:subtype_rejection_reason  → include/fixy/session/Subtype.h  — The trait class is gone. detail::subtype::reason_of derives the reason from subtype_verdict_v, and subtype_reason_t is its public name.
crucible/sessions/_SessionSubtypeReason.h:subtype_rejection_reason_t  → include/fixy/session/Subtype.h  — Renamed subtype_reason_t, which reads the verdict of the one walk.

# ── sessions: the event log ─────────────────────────────────────────────
crucible/sessions/_SessionEventLog.h:CheckpointId  — The new log writes no Checkpoint_Base or Checkpoint_Rollback record. Its coordinated checkpoint records carry a CheckpointRole and a StateHash of the saved state.
crucible/sessions/_SessionEventLog.h:CipherEventPayload  → include/fixy/session/EventLog.h  — The aggregate is gone. SessionEvent reads the four fields itself with cipher_content, cipher_timestamp_ns, cipher_from_tier and cipher_to_tier.
crucible/sessions/_SessionEventLog.h:default_payload_hash_fn  — The opt-in hasher is gone. The recorder of fixy/session/Recording.h writes the not-hashed sentinel, and a writer that wants a payload hash gives one to SessionEvent::send.
crucible/sessions/_SessionEventLog.h:StepIdKeyFn  → include/fixy/session/EventLog.h  — Renamed detail::event_log::StepProjection. Cipher.h orders its own log with this key, so its flip must name the projection or define its own key.
crucible/sessions/_SessionEventLog.h:StepIdLess  → include/fixy/session/EventLog.h  — Renamed detail::event_log::StepLess, the order that the log of SessionEventLog keeps. Cipher.h uses it for its own log, as it uses StepIdKeyFn.

# ── sessions: the checkpoint combinator ─────────────────────────────────
crucible/sessions/_SessionCheckpoint.h:assert_checkpointed_matches  — Dropped with CheckpointedSession, whose two arms it compared with two expected arms. A coordinated checkpoint has no pair of arms to compare.
crucible/sessions/_SessionCheckpoint.h:checkpoint_base  — Dropped with CheckpointedSession. The primitive Commit<K> names its continuation as Commit<K>::next, and Roll and Abort have no continuation.
crucible/sessions/_SessionCheckpoint.h:checkpoint_base_t  — Dropped with checkpoint_base. Commit<K>::next is the continuation after a checkpoint.
crucible/sessions/_SessionCheckpoint.h:Checkpointed  → include/fixy/session/Checkpoint.h  — The concept is gone. is_checkpoint_primitive recognizes Commit, Roll and Abort, the three labels that replace the combinator.
crucible/sessions/_SessionCheckpoint.h:CheckpointedSession  → include/fixy/session/Checkpoint.h  — Replaced by the labels Commit, Roll and Abort, which one party selects and the other party receives. The old combinator let each endpoint choose its arm alone.
crucible/sessions/_SessionCheckpoint.h:checkpoint_rollback  — Dropped with CheckpointedSession. Roll returns both parties to their last checkpoints, so no protocol names a rollback arm.
crucible/sessions/_SessionCheckpoint.h:checkpoint_rollback_t  — Dropped with checkpoint_rollback, for the same reason.
crucible/sessions/_SessionCheckpoint.h:is_checkpointed_session  → include/fixy/session/Checkpoint.h  — Replaced by is_checkpoint_primitive, which recognizes the three labels Commit, Roll and Abort.
crucible/sessions/_SessionCheckpoint.h:is_checkpointed_session_v  — Dropped with is_checkpointed_session. is_checkpoint_primitive has no variable form.
crucible/sessions/_SessionCheckpoint.h:is_dual_involutive  — Dropped with CheckpointedSession. dual_of maps Commit<K> to Commit of the dual of K and keeps Roll and Abort, so duality stays an involution when it is one on K.
crucible/sessions/_SessionCheckpoint.h:is_subtype_sync_structural  — Dropped with CheckpointedSession. The new tree states no subtype rule for a checkpoint protocol, and mint_checkpoint_session checks the pair with checkpoint_compliant_v.

# ── sessions: crash-stop and the crash transport ────────────────────────
crucible/bridges/_CrashTransport.h:CrashEvent  — Dropped. On a crash the decorator of fixy/session/CrashTransport.h runs the declared crash branch, whose head Recv<Crash<Peer>, K> gives the crash record.
crucible/bridges/_CrashTransport.h:CrashEventMatchesSurvivors  — Dropped with CrashEvent and with permission inheritance, which has no carrier.
crucible/bridges/_CrashTransport.h:CrashWatchedHandle  → include/fixy/session/CrashTransport.h  — Replaced by CrashWatched, one decorator for every head. The old class had one specialization for each head, six in all.
crucible/bridges/_CrashTransport.h:mint_crash_watched_session  → include/fixy/session/CrashTransport.h  — Replaced by mint_crash_session, whose gate CrashSessionAdmissible also reads the set of reliable roles.
crucible/bridges/_CrashTransport.h:on_crash  — Dropped. It ran a handler on the error that a crashed handle returned, and a crash now runs the crash branch of the protocol.
crucible/bridges/_CrashTransport.h:wrap_crash_return  — Dropped. It built the CrashEvent that a crashed handle returned, and the new decorator returns no error on a crash.
crucible/bridges/_CrashTransport.h:WrapCrashReturnKey  → include/fixy/session/CrashTransport.h  — The passkey of wrap_crash_return, which is dropped. The new crash transport has a session door, CrashSessionDoor, instead.
crucible/sessions/_SessionCrash.h:assert_every_offer_has_crash_branch_for  → include/fixy/session/CrashTransport.h  — Folded into CrashSessionAdmissible, the gate of mint_crash_session, which requires every_reception_handles_crash_v.
crucible/sessions/_SessionCrash.h:assert_has_crash_branch_for  — Dropped. The gate of mint_crash_session checks every reception of the protocol, so no check for one Offer is necessary.
crucible/sessions/_SessionCrash.h:CrashClass  → include/fixy/session/Crash.h  — The using-declaration re-exported the crash class of _CrashLattice.h. The new tree records how a peer stopped as CrashCause, which orders nothing.
crucible/sessions/_SessionCrash.h:CrashLattice  — The using-declaration re-exported the crash lattice. The new tree has no crash lattice, because the crash cause is metadata and not a grade.
crucible/sessions/_SessionCrash.h:every_offer_has_crash_branch_for_peer_v  → include/fixy/session/Crash.h  — Replaced by every_reception_handles_crash_v, which also reads the reliable set and refuses a bare Recv from an unreliable peer.
crucible/sessions/_SessionCrash.h:has_crash_branch_for_peer  — Dropped. every_reception_handles_crash_v checks each reception of the protocol, so no check for one Offer is necessary.
crucible/sessions/_SessionCrash.h:has_crash_branch_for_peer_v  — Dropped with has_crash_branch_for_peer, for the same reason.
crucible/sessions/_SessionCrash.h:is_crash  → include/fixy/session/Crash.h  — Renamed is_crash_payload.
crucible/sessions/_SessionCrash.h:is_crash_v  → include/fixy/session/Crash.h  — Renamed is_crash_payload_v.
crucible/sessions/_SessionCrash.h:is_dual_involutive  — Dropped. Stop is registered with the protocol registry as self-dual, and fixy/session/Protocol.h makes duality an involution on each registered combinator.
crucible/sessions/_SessionCrash.h:is_reliable  → include/fixy/session/Crash.h  — Renamed is_in_reliable_set.
crucible/sessions/_SessionCrash.h:is_reliable_v  — Dropped with is_reliable. is_in_reliable_set has no variable form.
crucible/sessions/_SessionCrash.h:is_subtype_sync_structural  — Dropped. The old rule made Stop the bottom of the subtype order, and the new registration relates Stop only to itself, as rule Sub-stop says.
crucible/sessions/_SessionCrash.h:Stop_g  → include/fixy/session/Crash.h  — Replaced by Stop, which carries no crash class. The calculus has no crash classes, and the crash cause travels on the crash record.
crucible/sessions/_SessionCrash.h:UnreliableAll  → include/fixy/session/Crash.h  — Renamed NoReliableRoles, the empty ReliableSet.

# ── bridges: the session recorders ──────────────────────────────────────
crucible/bridges/_RecordingPermissionedSessionHandle.h:mint_recording_session  → include/fixy/session/Recording.h  — Replaced by mint_recorded_session, which wraps any handle that RecordableHandle admits and opens through RecordingDoor.
crucible/bridges/_RecordingPermissionedSessionHandle.h:RecordingPermissionedSessionHandle  → include/fixy/session/Recording.h  — Folded into Recorded. The new SessionHandle carries its permission set as a parameter, so one decorator records a permissioned handle and a plain handle.

crucible/bridges/_RecordingSessionHandle.h:RecordingSessionHandle  → include/fixy/session/Recording.h  — Replaced by Recorded, one decorator over a plain handle, a crash-watched handle or a checkpoint handle.

crucible/sessions/_SessionMint.h:CtxFitsChannel  → include/fixy/session/Handle.h  — Replaced by CtxFitsForkedChannel. The new channel mint forks the two sides from one context, so the gate checks the protocol and its dual against that one context.

crucible/sessions/_Session.h:is_dual_involutive  — Dropped. fixy/session/Protocol.h makes duality an involution on each registered combinator, and a Select or an Offer keeps its note under duality.

crucible/sessions/_SessionGlobal.h:plain_merge_t  → include/fixy/session/Projection.h  — Replaced by the full merge of rule P-merge. Two internal choices merge when they have the same labels, and two external choices merge into the union of their labels.
crucible/sessions/_SessionGlobal.h:Project  → include/fixy/session/Projection.h  — Replaced by project_t and project_crash_t. No caller specializes a class template to project a subtree under a different root.
crucible/sessions/_SessionGlobal.h:Rec_G  → include/fixy/session/Global.h  — Replaced by global::Rec.
crucible/sessions/_SessionGlobal.h:RoleList  → include/fixy/session/Global.h  — Replaced by global::Roles.
crucible/sessions/_SessionGlobal.h:RolesOf  → include/fixy/session/Global.h  — Replaced by roles_t, an alias over a walk that inserts each role one time.
crucible/sessions/_SessionGlobal.h:roles_of_t  → include/fixy/session/Global.h  — Replaced by roles_t.
crucible/sessions/_SessionGlobal.h:StopG  → include/fixy/session/Global.h  — Replaced by the crash branch Branch<CrashLabel, void, Cont> and the runtime annotation Crashed<Role> of Barwell, Hou, Yoshida and Zhou. The crash class has no carrier in a global type.
crucible/sessions/_SessionGlobal.h:Transmission  → include/fixy/session/Global.h  — Replaced by global::Msg<From, To, Label, Payload, Cont>, the form of global::Comm with one branch. Each message carries a label.
crucible/sessions/_SessionGlobal.h:union_roles_t  — Dropped. The walks of fixy/session/Global.h union the role sets in its detail namespace, and no reader outside that header asks for a union.
crucible/sessions/_SessionGlobal.h:Var_G  → include/fixy/session/Global.h  — Replaced by global::Var.
crucible/sessions/_SessionQueue.h:count_matching  → include/fixy/session/Global.h  — Replaced by en_route_count_v, the count of en-route messages from one role to another in a global type (Definition 16).
crucible/sessions/_SessionQueue.h:count_matching_v  → include/fixy/session/Global.h  — Replaced by en_route_count_v.
crucible/sessions/_SessionQueue.h:dequeue_queue_t  → include/fixy/session/Semantics.h  — Replaced by the receive transition of config::step_t, which takes the first message to the receiver from the queue of its peer.
crucible/sessions/_SessionQueue.h:EmptyQueue  → include/fixy/session/Projection.h  — Replaced by OutQueue<>.
crucible/sessions/_SessionQueue.h:EnqueueQueue  → include/fixy/session/Semantics.h  — Replaced by the send transition of config::step_t, which appends the message to the queue of its sender.
crucible/sessions/_SessionQueue.h:enqueue_queue_t  → include/fixy/session/Semantics.h  — Replaced by the send transition of config::step_t.
crucible/sessions/_SessionQueue.h:HeadQueue  → include/fixy/session/Semantics.h  — Replaced by the receive transition of config::step_t. The transition exists only when the queue of the peer holds a message to the receiver.
crucible/sessions/_SessionQueue.h:head_queue_t  → include/fixy/session/Semantics.h  — Replaced by the receive transition of config::step_t.
crucible/sessions/_SessionQueue.h:is_bounded_queue_v  → include/fixy/session/Global.h  — Replaced by is_balanced_plus_v, which proves that the count of en-route messages exists for each pair of roles. A bound on one queue state proved nothing about a protocol.
crucible/sessions/_SessionQueue.h:is_queue  — Dropped. The association walk of fixy/session/Projection.h refuses a queue that is not an OutQueue of Queued elements, and no reader asks the question on its own.
crucible/sessions/_SessionQueue.h:is_queued_msg  — Dropped with is_queue.
crucible/sessions/_SessionQueue.h:is_queued_msg_v  — Dropped with is_queue.
crucible/sessions/_SessionQueue.h:is_queue_empty_v  — Dropped. A reader compares a queue with OutQueue<>, as the association rule for a finished role does.
crucible/sessions/_SessionQueue.h:is_queue_state_v  — Dropped. A queue is an OutQueue of fixy/session/Projection.h or an UnavailableQueue of fixy/session/Crash.h, and no reader asks for the union of the two.
crucible/sessions/_SessionQueue.h:is_queue_v  — Dropped with is_queue.
crucible/sessions/_SessionQueue.h:is_unavailable_queue_v  → include/fixy/session/Crash.h  — Replaced by is_unavailable_queue, whose value answers the same question.
crucible/sessions/_SessionQueue.h:Queue  → include/fixy/session/Projection.h  — Replaced by OutQueue<Queued...>, the outgoing messages of one role. Two queues are equivalent when they agree for each receiver.
crucible/sessions/_SessionQueue.h:queue_contains  — Dropped. The association walk compares two queues for each receiver, and no reader asks whether a queue holds a message for one pair of roles.
crucible/sessions/_SessionQueue.h:queue_contains_v  — Dropped with queue_contains.
crucible/sessions/_SessionQueue.h:QueuedMsg  → include/fixy/session/Projection.h  — Replaced by Queued<To, Label, Payload>. A queue belongs to its sender, so an element names its receiver and its label.
crucible/sessions/_SessionQueue.h:queue_size_v  → include/fixy/session/Projection.h  — Replaced by OutQueue::size.
crucible/sessions/_SessionQueue.h:TailQueue  → include/fixy/session/Semantics.h  — Replaced by the receive transition of config::step_t, which removes the message that the receiver takes.
crucible/sessions/_SessionQueue.h:tail_queue_t  → include/fixy/session/Semantics.h  — Replaced by the receive transition of config::step_t.
